/// Domain-specific errors for hyper-parameter tuning & tracking.\n    #[derive(Debug, Error)]\n    pub enum TunerError {\n        #[error(\"Unknown tuner strategy: {0}\")]\n }\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\nmod observer {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// Observable events emitted by the training loop.\n  },\n    }\n\n    /// Observer trait that other layers can implement to react to events.\n    pub trait TrainingObserver: Send + Sync {\n        fn on_event(&self, event: &TrainingEvent);\n ExperimentTracker {\n        pub fn new() -> Self {\n            // 32 is an arbitrary channel size; adjust to throughput needs.\n            let (tx, _) = broadcast::channel(32);\n            Self {\n                state: Arc::new(RwLock::new(HashMap::new())),\n                broadcaster: tx,\n            }\n        }\n\n        /// Subscribe to training events.\n        pub fn ExperimentRun {\n                run_id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n                params,\n                start_time: SystemTime::now(),\n                end_time: None,\n                metrics: HashMap::new(),\n                tags,\n            };\n            let run_id = run.run_id;\n            self.state.write().await.insert(run_id, run);\n            run_id\n        }\n\n        /// HashMap<String, f64>,\n        ) -> Result<(), TunerError> {\n            let mut state = self.state.write().await;\n            let run = state\n                .get_mut(&run_id)\n                .ok_or_else(|| TunerError::UnknownRun(run_id))?;\n\n            // Merge metrics into current map (overwrite duplicates).\n            for (k, v) in &metrics {\n                run.metrics.insert(k.clone(), *v);\n            }\n\n            let event = TrainingEvent::EpochEnd {\n                run_id,\n                epoch,\n                metrics,\n                timestamp: SystemTime::now(),\n            };\n            let _ = self.broadcaster.send(event); // Ignore if no receivers.\n            Ok(())\n        }\n\n        /// Finalize a run, compute its duration & send final event.\n        pub async fn end_run(\n            &self,\n            run_id: Uuid,\n            final_metrics: HashMap<String, f64>,\n        ) -> Result<(), TunerError> {\n run.end_time = Some(SystemTime::now());\n            for (k, v) in &final_metrics {\n                run.metrics.insert(k.clone(), *v);\n            }\n\n            let duration = run\n Int(i64),\n        Float(f64),\n        Text(String),\n        Flag(bool),\n    }\n\n    impl fmt::Display for HyperParamValue {\n        fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n f64 },\n        Choice { options: Vec<String> },\n        Flag,\n    }\n\n    // -------------------------- Strategy Trait ----------------------------\n    #[async_trait::async_trait]\n    pub trait  score: f64,\n        ) -> Result<(), TunerError>;\n\n        /// Optional JSON snapshot for auditability.\n        async fn snapshot(&self) -> Result<String, TunerError>;\n    }\n\n    // ---------------------- Random Search Strategy ------------------------\n    pub struct RandomSearchTuner {\n        specs: HashMap<String, HyperParamSpec>,\n        rng: RwLock<StdRng>,\n    }\n\n    impl RandomSearchTuner {\n        pub fn new(specs: HashMap<String, HyperParamSpec>) -> Self {\n            Self {\n                specs,\n                rng: RwLock::new(StdRng::from_entropy()),\n            }\n        }\n\n        fn sample_from_spec(\n            spec: &HyperParamSpec,\n            rng: &mut StdRng,\n        ) -> HyperParamValue {\n            match spec {\n                HyperParamSpec::IntRange { min, max } => {\n                    let dist = Uniform::new_inclusive(*min, *max);\n                    HyperParamValue::Int(rng.sample(dist))\n                }\n                HyperParamSpec::FloatRange { min, max } => {\n                    let dist = Uniform::new_inclusive(*min, *max);\n                    HyperParamValue::Float(rng.sample(dist))\n                }\n                HyperParamSpec::Choice { options } => {\n                    let idx = rng.gen_range(0..options.len());\n                    HyperParamValue::Text(options[idx].clone())\n                }\n                HyperParamSpec::Flag => HyperParamValue::Flag(rng.gen::<bool>()),\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[async_trait::async_trait]\n    impl HyperParamTuner for RandomSearchTuner {\n        async fn suggest(&self) -> HashMap<String, HyperParamValue> {\n            let mut rng = self.rng.write().await;\n            self.specs\n                .iter()\n                .map(|(k, spec)| (k.clone(), Self::sample_from_spec(spec, &mut rng)))\n                .collect()\n        }\n\n        async fn record_result(\n            &self,\n            _params: #[tokio::test]\n    async fn smoke_test_random_search_and_tracker() {\n        // 1. The channel is bounded to apply back-pressure in\n   228→    /// pathological cases.\n   229→    pub fn new(buffer: usize) -> Self {\n   230→        let observers = Arc::new(Mutex::new(Vec::new()));\n   231→        let (sender, receiver) = bounded::<ModelMetrics>(buffer);\n   232→        let observers_clone = observers.clone();\n   233→\n   234→        let handle = thread::spawn(move || Self::run(receiver, observers_clone));\n   235→\n   236→        Self {\n   237→            observers,\n   238→            sender,\n   239→            _worker_handle: handle,\n   240→        }\n   241→    }\n   242→\n   243→    /// Internal worker loop.\n   244→    fn run(receiver: Receiver<ModelMetrics>, observers: Arc<Mutex<Vec<Arc<dyn MetricsObserver>>>>) {\n   245→        for metrics in receiver.iter() {\n   246→            debug!(\"MetricMonitor received metrics: {:?}\", metrics);\n   247→            let snapshot = {\n   248→                // Take a snapshot to minimise lock contention duration.\n   249→                observers.lock().unwrap().clone()\n   250→            };\n   251→            for obs in snapshot {\n   252→                obs.on_metrics(metrics.clone());\n   253→            }\n   254→        }\n   255→        info!(\"MetricMonitor worker terminating\");\n   256→    }\n   257→\n   258→    /// Register a new observer.\n   259→    pub fn subscribe<O: MetricsObserver + 'static>(&self, observer: O) {\n   260→        self.observers.lock().unwrap().push(Arc::new(observer));\n   261→    }\n   262→\n   263→    /// Feed metrics into the monitor. Called by the system’s telemetry layer.\n   264→    pub fn ingest(&self, metrics: ModelMetrics) {\n   265→        if let Err(e) = self.sender.send(metrics) {\n   266→            // Inbound side; losing metrics is severe – always log an error.\n   267→            error!(\"Failed to dispatch metrics: {}\", e);\n   268→        }\n   269→    }\n   270→}\n   271→\n   272→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   273→/*                  Observer Implementation: Retrain Trigger                  */\n   274→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   275→\n   276→/// Observer that decides when to trigger re-training based on metrics and\n   277→/// delegates hyper-parameter suggestion to an injected tuner (Strategy).\n   278→pub struct RetrainTrigger<T: HyperParameterTuner> {\n   279→    tuner: Mutex<T>,\n   280→    model_ops: Arc<dyn ModelOps>,\n   281→    /// Minimal gap (in seconds) between consecutive retraining events for the\n   282→    /// same model. Adaptive Hyper-parameter Tuning and Automated Re-training\n//!\n//! This module demonstrates how VisuTility Orchestrator performs on-the-fly\n//! hyper-parameter tuning and automated re-training while respecting the\n//! Strategy and Observer patterns.\n//\n//  ┌───────────────────────────┐\n//  │ MetricMonitor (Subject)   │\n//  └────────────┬──────────────┘\n//               │ notifies\n//  ┌────────────▼──────────────┐\n//  │ RetrainTrigger (Observer) │──────────────┐\n//  └────────────┬──────────────┘              │ calls\n//               │                             │\n//  ┌────────────▼──────────────┐              │\n//  │  HyperparameterTuner      │              │\n//  │  (Strategy ‑ pluggable)   │              │\n//  └────────────┬──────────────┘              │\n// prelude {\n    pub use super::{\n        GridSearchTuner, HyperParamSpace, HyperParameterTuner, HyperParameters, MetricMonitor,\n        ModelMetrics, ModelOps, RetrainTrigger,\n    };\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                                Domain Types                                */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Generic representation of a model’s hyper-parameters.\n///\n/// Internally we store a `serde_json::Value` for serde_json::Value,\n}\n\nimpl HyperParameters {\n    pub fn new(inner: serde_json::Value) -> Self {\n        Self { inner }\n    }\n\n    /// Convenience helper to get a numeric parameter.\n    pub fn get_f64(&self, key: &str) -> Option<f64> {\n        self.inner\n            .get(key)\n            .and_then(|v| v.as_f64())\n    }\n\n    /// Convenience helper to set/update a parameter.\n    pub fn set<K: Into<String>, T: Serialize>(&mut self, key: K, value: T) -> Result<()> {\n        let mut map = self\n            .inner\n            .as_object()\n            .cloned()\n            .unwrap_or_else(|| serde_json::Map::new());\n        map.insert(\n            key.into(),\n            serde_json::to_value(value).context(\"serializing parameter\")?,\n        );\n        self.inner = serde_json::Value::Object(map);\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// Metrics reported by a running model.\n///\n/// For brevity we only capture a handful of metrics. In production, metrics\n/// would be routed from Prometheus/OpenTelemetry exporters.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct ModelMetrics {\n    pub model_id: String,\n    pub accuracy: f64,\n    pub precision: f64,\n    pub recall: f64,\n    pub throughput_fps: f64,\n}\n\n/// Abstract capabilities that the Model-Ops layer must expose so that\n/// hyper-parameter tuning and re-training can be orchestrated independently\n/// of the underlying ML framework (e.g. TensorFlow, PyTorch, ONNX-Runtime).\npub trait ModelOps: Send + Sync {\n    /// Kick off an asynchronous re-training job for `model_id` using the\n    /// supplied hyper-parameters. The implementation is responsible for\n    /// returning immediately (non-blocking) and for emitting its own events\n    /// (status, success, failure) through the system’s event bus.\n    fn retrain(&self, model_id: &str, params: HyperParameters) -> Result<()>;\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                          Strategy: Hyper-parameter Tuner                   */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Search space descriptor for naïve grid-search.\n///\n/// More advanced tuners (e.g. Bayesian) would define their own descriptor.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct HyperParamSpace {\n    pub lr: Vec<f64>,\n    pub batch_size: Vec<u32>,\n    pub dropout: Vec<f64>,\n}\n\n/// Strategy interface for selecting the next candidate hyper-parameters.\npub trait HyperParameterTuner: Send + Sync {\n    /// Returns a new set of hyper-parameters based on the latest model metrics.\n    fn suggest(&mut self, metrics: &ModelMetrics) -> Option<HyperParameters>;\n}\n\n/// Reference strategy: exhaustive grid-search with random walk.\n///\n/// In production we would enumerate deterministically; here we randomise to\n/// avoid massive search space exploration in the example code.\npub struct GridSearchTuner {\n    /// Search grid catalogued in a naive way. For large search spaces, a\n    /// smarter iterator would be required.\n    grid: Vec<HyperParameters>,\n    /// Keep track of which configurations have been tried.\n    tried: HashMap<String, ()>,\n}\n\nimpl GridSearchTuner {\n    pub fn new(space: HyperParamSpace) -> Self {\n        let mut grid = Vec::new();\n        for lr in space.lr {\n            for batch in &space.batch_size {\n                for dropout in &space.dropout {\n                    let params = serde_json::json!({\n                        \"learning_rate\": lr,\n                        \"batch_size\": batch,\n                        \"dropout\": dropout,\n                    });\n                    grid.push(HyperParameters::new(params));\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        Self {\n            grid,\n            tried: HashMap::new(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl HyperParameterTuner for GridSearchTuner {\n    fn suggest(&mut self, metrics: &ModelMetrics) -> Option<HyperParameters> {\n        // If the model performs well enough, no need to tune.\n        if metrics.accuracy >= 0.97 {\n            info!(\n                \"Model [{}] already meets accuracy target ({}). Skipping suggestion.\",\n                metrics.model_id, metrics.accuracy\n            );\n            return None;\n        }\n\n        // Otherwise, randomly take an unseen configuration from the grid.\n        let mut rng = thread_rng();\n        self.grid.shuffle(&mut rng);\n\n        for params in &self.grid {\n            let fingerprint = serde_json::to_string(&params).ok()?;\n            if !self.tried.contains_key(&fingerprint) {\n                info!(\n                    \"Suggesting new hyper-parameters for model [{}]: {}\",\n                    metrics.model_id, fingerprint\n                );\n                self.tried.insert(fingerprint, ());\n                return Some(params.clone());\n            }\n        }\n\n        info!(\n            \"Grid exhausted for model [{}]; no more hyper-parameters to try.\",\n            metrics.model_id\n        );\n        None\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                       Observer: Metric-driven Re-trainer                   */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// A generic observer that reacts to model metrics.\n///\n/// In a real-world system this trait would likely come from a common\n/// crate so that multiple modules can subscribe to events uniformly.\npub trait MetricsObserver: Send + Sync {\n    fn on_metrics(&self, metrics: ModelMetrics);\n}\n\n/// Central hub that receives incoming metrics and dispatches them to\n/// registered observers. This plays the *Subject* role in the Observer pattern.\npub struct MetricMonitor {\n    observers: Arc<Mutex<Vec<Arc<dyn MetricsObserver>>>>,\n    sender: Sender<ModelMetrics>,\n    _worker_handle: thread::JoinHandle<()>,\n}\n\nimpl MetricMonitor {\n    /// Creates a new monitor with a background worker thread that forwards\n    /// metrics to observers. The channel is bounded to apply back-pressure in\n    /// pathological cases.\n    pub fn new(buffer: usize) -> Self {\n        let observers = Arc::new(Mutex::new(Vec::new()));\n        let (sender, receiver) = bounded::<ModelMetrics>(buffer);\n        let observers_clone = observers.clone();\n\n        let handle = thread::spawn(move || Self::run(receiver, observers_clone));\n\n        Self {\n            observers,\n            sender,\n            _worker_handle: handle,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Internal worker loop.\n    fn run(receiver: Receiver<ModelMetrics>, observers: Arc<Mutex<Vec<Arc<dyn MetricsObserver>>>>) {\n        for metrics in receiver.iter() {\n            debug!(\"MetricMonitor received metrics: {:?}\", metrics);\n            let snapshot = {\n                // Take a snapshot to minimise lock contention duration.\n                observers.lock().unwrap().clone()\n            };\n            for obs in snapshot {\n                obs.on_metrics(metrics.clone());\n            }\n        }\n        info!(\"MetricMonitor worker terminating\");\n    }\n\n    /// Register a new observer.\n    pub fn subscribe<O: MetricsObserver + 'static>(&self, observer: O) {\n        self.observers.lock().unwrap().push(Arc::new(observer));\n    }\n\n    /// Feed metrics into the monitor. Called by the system’s telemetry layer.\n    pub fn ingest(&self, metrics: ModelMetrics) {\n        if let Err(e) = self.sender.send(metrics) {\n            // Inbound side; losing metrics is severe – always log an error.\n            error!(\"Failed to dispatch metrics: {}\", e);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                  Observer Implementation: Retrain Trigger                  */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Observer that decides when to trigger re-training based on metrics and\n/// delegates hyper-parameter suggestion to an injected tuner (Strategy).\npub struct RetrainTrigger<T: HyperParameterTuner> {\n    tuner: Mutex<T>,\n    model_ops: Arc<dyn ModelOps>,\n    /// Minimal gap (in seconds) between consecutive retraining events for the\n    /// same model. Prevents runaway retraining loops.\n    cooldown_secs: u64,\n    /// Track last retrain timestamp per model.\n    last_retrain: Mutex<HashMap<String, std::time::Instant>>,\n}\n\nimpl<T> RetrainTrigger<T>\nwhere\n    T: HyperParameterTuner + 'static,\n{\n    pub fn new(tuner: T, model_ops: Arc<dyn ModelOps>, cooldown_secs: u64) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            tuner: Mutex::new(tuner),\n            model_ops,\n            cooldown_secs,\n            last_retrain: Mutex::new(HashMap::new()),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<T> MetricsObserver for RetrainTrigger<T>\nwhere\n    T: HyperParameterTuner + 'static,\n{\n    fn on_metrics(&self, metrics: ModelMetrics) {\n        let now = std::time::Instant::now();\n\n        // Respect cooldown.\n        {\n            let mut map = self.last_retrain.lock().unwrap();\n            if let Some(last) = map.get(&metrics.model_id) {\n                if now.duration_since(*last).as_secs() < self.cooldown_secs {\n                    debug!(\n                        \"Cooldown active for model [{}], skipping retrain\",\n                        metrics.model_id\n                    );\n                    return;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Ask the strategy for new hyper-parameters.\n        let maybe_params = {\n            let mut tuner = self.tuner.lock().unwrap();\n            tuner.suggest(&metrics)\n        };\n\n        if let Some(params) = maybe_params {\n            info!(\n                \"Triggering re-training for model [{}] with new parameters\",\n                metrics.model_id\n            );\n            match self.model_ops.retrain(&metrics.model_id, params) {\n                Ok(_) => {\n                    self.last_retrain\n                        .lock()\n                        .unwrap()\n                        .insert(metrics.model_id.clone(), now);\n                    info!(\"Re-training job submitted successfully\");\n                }\n                Err(e) => {\n                    error!(\"Failed to submit re-training job: {:?}\", e);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                       Minimal Mock Implementations                         */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// A stub implementation that simulates a long-running re-training job.\n/// Useful for smoke-testing the observer without touching external systems.\npub struct MockModelOps;\n\nimpl ModelOps for MockModelOps {\n    fn retrain(&self, model_id: &str, params: HyperParameters) -> Result<()> {\n        let info!(\n                \"Retraining [{}] in background with params: {}\",\n                id, fingerprint\n            );\n            thread::sleep(Duration::from_secs(3));\n            info!(\"Retraining [{}] finished\", id);\n        });\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/* 102→}\n   103→\n   104→impl Default for HyperParams {\n   105→    fn default() -> Self {\n   106→        Self {\n   107→            learning_rate: 3e-4,\n   108→            weight_decay: 1e-2,\n 116→#[async_trait]\n   117→pub trait HyperparameterTuner: Send + Sync {\n   118→    /// Consume recent metrics & propose a new hyper-parameters set.\n   119→    async fn propose(\n   120→        &mut returns a weighted sampling distribution over\n   208→    /// previous params (higher accuracy → higher weight).\n   209→    fn weighted_sample(&mut self) -> Option<HyperParams> {\n   210→        if self.history.is_empty() {\n   211→            return None;\n   212→        }\n   213→        // Build CDF based on accuracy.\n   214→        let total_acc: f32 = self.history.iter().map(|(_, acc)| acc).sum();\n   215→        if total_acc == 0.0 {\n   216→            return None;\n   217→        }\n   218→        let mut rng_val: f32 = rand::random();\n   219→        for (params, acc) in self.history.iter() {\n   220→            rng_val -= *acc / total_acc;\n   221→            if rng_val <= 0.0 {\n   222→                return Some(params.clone());\n   223→            }\n   224→ propose(\n   232→        &mut self,\n   233→        latest_metrics: &ModelMetrics,\n   234→    ) -> Result<HyperParams> {\n   235→        // Persist the last known good params and accuracy for future sampling.\n   236→        if let Some(prev) = self.history.back() {\n   237→            // Only store if the model is improving and the change is meaningful.\n   238→            if latest_metrics.accuracy > prev.1 + 0.002 {\n   239→                let mut entry = prev.clone();\n   240→                entry.1 = latest_metrics.accuracy;\n   241→ Module 53 – Hyper-Parameter Tuning & Monitoring Integration\n//! ----------------------------------------------------------\n//! This module glues together three major concerns in the VisuTility Orchestrator:\n//! 1. Live collection of model–performance metrics coming from Serving-Ops.\n//! 2. Observer-Pattern broadcast of those metrics to interested components.\n//! 3. Strategy-Pattern hyper-parameter tuning algorithms that leverage the\n//!    latest metrics to propose improved configurations.\n//!\n//! The module purposefully stays generic (no hard-coded CV model details) so it\n//! can be reused by any vision model registered in the internal Model-Registry.\n//!\n//! Key concepts implemented below:\n//! • ModelMetrics          – Lightweight, serializable metrics snapshot.\n//! • MetricsPublisher      – Observable broadcaster (tokio::broadcast).\n//! • HyperparameterTuner   – Strategy trait with async proposal interface.\n//! • GridTuner/BayesianTuner – Two concrete strategy implementations.\n//! • TunerFactory          – Factory-Pattern for runtime strategy selection.\n//! • AutoTuningService     – End-to-end orchestrator that wires everything up.\n//!\n//! Author: VisuTility Core Team\n//! License: Apache-2.0\n\nuse std::{\n    collections::{HashMap, VecDeque},\n    sync::Arc,\n    time::{Duration, Instant},\n};\n\nuse anyhow::{anyhow, Context, Result};\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse log::{debug, error, info, warn};\nuse rand::{distributions::Uniform, prelude::Distribution, rngs::ThreadRng};\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse tokio::sync::{broadcast, Mutex};\nuse tokio::time;\n\n/// A minimal set of model-quality metrics we care about during tuning.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct ModelMetrics {\n    /// Overall model accuracy over the most recent validation window.\n    pub accuracy: f32,\n    /// Mean inference latency in milliseconds.\n    pub latency_ms: f32,\n    /// Timestamp when the metrics were recorded.\n    pub captured_at: Instant,\n}\n\nimpl ModelMetrics {\n    pub fn is_good_enough(&self, min_accuracy: f32, max_latency: f32) -> bool {\n        self.accuracy >= min_accuracy && self.latency_ms <= max_latency\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                       Observer Pattern – Metrics Bus                       */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Buffer size used for the broadcast channel.\nconst METRICS_BUFFER: usize = 128;\n\n/// Component that publishes `ModelMetrics` events so that any number of\n/// subscribers can react (e.g. dashboards, alerting, tuners, auditors).\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct MetricsPublisher {\n    inner: broadcast::Sender<ModelMetrics>,\n}\n\nimpl MetricsPublisher {\n    /// Create a new publisher / broadcast channel.\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        let (tx, _rx) = broadcast::channel(METRICS_BUFFER);\n        Self { inner: tx }\n    }\n\n    /// Send a metrics snapshot to all subscribers, logging on error.\n    pub fn publish(&self, metrics: ModelMetrics) {\n        if let Err(err) = self.inner.send(metrics) {\n            warn!(\"MetricsPublisher: failed to broadcast metrics: {err}\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Register a new subscriber for the metrics stream.\n    pub fn subscribe(&self) -> broadcast::Receiver<ModelMetrics> {\n        self.inner.subscribe()\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*              Strategy Pattern – Hyper-parameter Tuning Trait              */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Canonical list of tunable hyper-parameters for computer-vision models.\n/// This is intentionally generic; individual model adapters perform mapping\n/// between these and their low-level framework/graph parameters.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq)]\npub struct HyperParams {\n    pub learning_rate: f32,\n    pub weight_decay: f32,\n    pub batch_size: usize,\n    pub augmentation_prob: f32,\n}\n\nimpl Default for HyperParams {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            learning_rate: 3e-4,\n            weight_decay: 1e-2,\n            batch_size: 32,\n            augmentation_prob: 0.5,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Behaviour that each tuning strategy must implement.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait HyperparameterTuner: Send + Sync {\n    /// Consume recent metrics & propose a new hyper-parameters set.\n    async fn propose(\n        &mut self,\n        latest_metrics: &ModelMetrics,\n    ) -> Result<HyperParams>;\n\n    /// Optional hook so that external orchestration code can name the strategy.\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str;\n}\n\n/* ----------------------- Concrete Strategy: Grid Search ------------------- */\n\n/// Very small fixed search-space grid-search tuner.\n/// Good as a baseline and for deterministic troubleshooting sessions.\npub struct GridTuner {\n    grid: Vec<HyperParams>,\n    cursor: usize,\n}\n\nimpl GridTuner {\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        // Build a tiny but combinatorically diverse grid.\n        let learning_rates = vec![1e-3, 3e-4, 1e-4];\n        let weight_decays = vec![1e-1, 1e-2];\n        let batch_sizes = vec![16, 32, 64];\n        let aug_probs = vec![0.3, 0.5, 0.8];\n\n        let mut grid = Vec::new();\n        for lr in &learning_rates {\n            for wd in &weight_decays {\n                for bs in &batch_sizes {\n                    for ap in &aug_probs {\n                        grid.push(HyperParams {\n                            learning_rate: *lr,\n                            weight_decay: *wd,\n                            batch_size: *bs,\n                            augmentation_prob: *ap,\n                        });\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        Self { grid, cursor: 0 }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl HyperparameterTuner for GridTuner {\n    async fn propose(\n        &mut self,\n        _latest_metrics: &ModelMetrics,\n    ) -> Result<HyperParams> {\n        if self.cursor >= self.grid.len() {\n            return Err(anyhow!(\"GridTuner has exhausted its search space\"));\n        }\n        let proposal = self.grid[self.cursor].clone();\n        self.cursor += 1;\n        Ok(proposal)\n    }\n\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"GridTuner\"\n    }\n}\n\n/* --------------------- Concrete Strategy: Bayesian Tuner ------------------ */\n\n/// Crude Bayesian optimisation tuner using a Gaussian-like exploration.\n/// In production we would leverage a dedicated crate (e.g., `bayes_opt`),\n/// but for this reference implementation we keep it self-contained.\npub struct BayesianTuner {\n    rng: ThreadRng,\n    /// History of tried params and their resulting accuracy so we can\n    /// bias towards the best ones (simplified Thompson Sampling).\n    history: VecDeque<(HyperParams, f32 /* accuracy */)>,\n    /// Maximum amount of history entries to keep in memory.\n    history_capacity: usize,\n}\n\nimpl BayesianTuner {\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            rng: rand::thread_rng(),\n            history: VecDeque::new(),\n            history_capacity: 200,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Internal helper that returns a weighted sampling distribution over\n    /// previous params (higher accuracy → higher weight).\n    fn weighted_sample(&mut self) -> Option<HyperParams> {\n        if self.history.is_empty() {\n            return None;\n        }\n        // Build CDF based on accuracy.\n        let total_acc: f32 = self.history.iter().map(|(_, acc)| acc).sum();\n        if total_acc == 0.0 {\n            return None;\n        }\n        let mut rng_val: f32 = rand::random();\n        for (params, acc) in self.history.iter() {\n            rng_val -= *acc / total_acc;\n            if rng_val <= 0.0 {\n                return Some(params.clone());\n            }\n        }\n        self.history.back().map(|(p, _)| p.clone())\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl HyperparameterTuner for BayesianTuner {\n    async fn propose(\n        &mut self,\n        latest_metrics: &ModelMetrics,\n    ) -> Result<HyperParams> {\n        // Persist the last known good params and accuracy for future sampling.\n        if let Some(prev) = self.history.back() {\n            // Only store if the model is improving and the change is meaningful.\n            if latest_metrics.accuracy > prev.1 + 0.002 {\n                let mut entry = prev.clone();\n                entry.1 = latest_metrics.accuracy;\n                self.history.push_back(entry);\n                if self.history.len() > self.history_capacity {\n                    self.history.pop_front();\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Either sample near a historically good configuration or sample random.\n        let base = self.weighted_sample().unwrap_or_else(|| HyperParams::default());\n\n        // Jitter around the base parameters.\n        let lr_dist = Uniform::new(0.5, 1.5);\n        let wd_dist = Uniform::new(0.5, 1.5);\n        let aug_dist = Uniform::new(-0.1f32, 0.1f32);\n\n        let mut proposal = base.clone();\n        proposal.learning_rate *= lr_dist.sample(&mut self.rng);\n        proposal.weight_decay *= wd_dist.sample(&mut self.rng);\n        proposal.augmentation_prob =\n            (proposal.augmentation_prob + aug_dist.sample(&mut self.rng)).clamp(0.0, 1.0);\n        // Pick a nearby batch size (±1 step).\n        proposal.batch_size = match self.rng.gen_range(0..=2) {\n            0 if proposal.batch_size > 8 => proposal.batch_size / 2,\n            2 => proposal.batch_size * 2,\n            _ => proposal.batch_size,\n        }\n        .clamp(8, 256);\n\n        Ok(proposal)\n    }\n\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"BayesianTuner\"\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                           Factory Pattern – Builder                        */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Enumeration of all supported tuner implementations.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]\npub enum TunerType {\n    Grid,\n    Bayesian,\n}\n\npub struct TunerFactory;\n\nimpl TunerFactory {\n    pub fn build(tuner: TunerType) -> Arc<Mutex<dyn HyperparameterTuner>> {\n        match tuner {\n            TunerType::Grid => Arc::new(Mutex::new(GridTuner::new())),\n            TunerType::Bayesian => Arc::new(Mutex::new(BayesianTuner::new())),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                 Auto-Tuning Service – End-to-End Orchestrator              */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Interval between automatic tuning rounds (default = 30 minutes).\nconst DEFAULT_TUNING_INTERVAL: Duration = Duration::from_secs(30 * 60);\n\n/// Service that connects the metrics stream with the tuner and emits new\n/// hyper-parameters whenever the strategy decides an update is needed.\n/// The produced configs are sent back to Model-Ops to trigger re-training.\npub struct AutoTuningService {\n    metrics_sub: broadcast::Receiver<ModelMetrics>,\n    tuner: Arc<Mutex<dyn HyperparameterTuner>>,\n    min_accuracy: f32,\n    max_latency_ms: f32,\n    interval: Duration,\n}\n\nimpl AutoTuningService {\n    /// Construct and wire the background service.\n    pub fn new(\n        publisher: MetricsPublisher,\n        tuner: Arc<Mutex<dyn HyperparameterTuner>>,\n        min_accuracy: f32,\n        max_latency_ms: f32,\n    ) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            metrics_sub: publisher.subscribe(),\n            tuner,\n            min_accuracy,\n            max_latency_ms,\n            interval: DEFAULT_TUNING_INTERVAL,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Adjustable interval mainly used in integration tests.\n    pub fn with_interval(mut self, interval: Duration) -> Self {\n        self.interval = interval;\n        self\n    }\n\n    /// Spawn the service in its own asynchronous task. The join-handle is\n    /// returned so the caller can manage lifecycle (e.g. cancel on shutdown).\n    pub fn spawn(self) -> tokio::task::JoinHandle<()> {\n        tokio::spawn(async move { self.run().await })\n    }\n\n    async fn run(mut self) {\n        let mut ticker = time::interval(self.interval);\n        let mut latest_metrics: Option<ModelMetrics> = None;\n\n        loop {\n            tokio::select! {\n                biased;\n\n                _ = ticker.tick() => {\n                    if let Some(metrics) = &latest_metrics {\n                        if metrics.is_good_enough(self.min_accuracy, self.max_latency_ms) {\n                            debug!(\"AutoTuningService: metrics already above threshold – skip round\");\n                            continue;\n                        }\n                        info!(\"AutoTuningService: triggering tuner `{}`\", self.tuner.lock().await.name());\n                        match self.tuner.lock().await.propose(metrics).await {\n                            Ok(new_params) => {\n                                // In production, we would invoke Model-Ops here.\n                                info!(\n                                    \"AutoTuningService: proposed new params: lr={:.5}, wd={:.5}, bs={}, aug={:.2}\",\n                                    new_params.learning_rate,\n                                    new_params.weight_decay,\n                                    new_params.batch_size,\n                                    new_params.augmentation_prob,\n                                );\n                                // TODO: send to training scheduler via gRPC/kafka/etc.\n                            }\n                            Err(err) => {\n                                error!(\"AutoTuningService: tuner error: {err:#}\");\n                            }\n                        }\n                    } else {\n                        debug!(\"AutoTuningService: no metrics yet – waiting\");\n                    }\n                }\n\n                // Receive metrics asynchronously; ignore errors when channel is lagging.\n                res = self.metrics_sub.recv() => match res {\n                    Ok(m) => {\n                        latest_metrics = Some(m);\n                    }\n    Err(broadcast::error::RecvError::Closed) => {\n                        warn!(\"AutoTuningService: metrics channel closed – terminating\");\n                        return;\n                    }\n   232→    /// Computes moving average loss over the internal window.\n   233→    fn avg_loss(&self) -> Option<f32> {\n   234→        if self.history.is_empty() {\n   235→            return None;\n   236→        }\n   237→        Some(\n   238→            self.history\n   239→                .iter()\n   240→                .map(|m| m.loss)\n   241→                .sum::<f32>()\n   242→                / self.history.len() as f32,\n   243→        )\n   244→    }\n   245→}\n   246→\n   247→impl MetricsObserver for PerformanceMonitor {\n   248→    fn on_metrics_update(&mut self, metrics: {\n   319→                    recv(self.rx_metrics) -> msg => {\n   320→                        match msg {\n   321→                            Ok((cfg, metrics)) => {\n   322→                                // 1) Produce new config via tuner.\n   323→                                let mut tuner = self.tuner.lock().expect(\"poisoned lock\");\n   324→                                match tuner.tune(&cfg, &metrics) {\n   325→                                    Ok(new_cfg) => {\n   326→                                        // Send back to upstream training service\n   327→                                        if let Err(err) = self.tx_config.send(new_cfg) {\n   328→                                            eprintln!(\"TuningCoordinator: failed to send new config: {err}\");\n   329→                                        }\n   330→                                    }\n   331→                                    Err(e) => eprintln!(\"TuningCoordinator: tune() failed: {e:?}\"),\n   332→                                }\n   333→\n   334→                                // 2) Notify observers.\n   335→                                for obs in self.observers.lock().expect(\"poisoned lock\").iter_mut() {\n   336→                                    obs.on_metrics_update(&metrics);\n   337→                                }\n   338→                            }\n   339→                            Err(_) => {\n   340→                                // Upstream closed.                 }\n   349→                }\n   350→            }\n   351→        });\n   352→    }\n   353→}\n   354→\n   355→//\n   356→// ---------- Self-contained Integration Test ThreadRng,\n    exploration_rate: f64,\n}\n\nimpl BayesianTuner {\n    pub fn new(exploration_rate: f64) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            rng: thread_rng(),\n            exploration_rate,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl HyperparameterTuner for BayesianTuner {\n    fn strategy_name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"bayesian_search\"\n    }\n\n    fn tune(\n        &mut self,\n        last_config: &HyperParameterConfig,\n        _last_metrics: &TrainingMetrics,\n    ) -> TunerResult<HyperParameterConfig> {\n        let mut new_cfg = last_config.clone();\n        for value in new_cfg.params.values_mut() {\n            let noise: f64 = self.rng.gen::<f64>() * self.exploration_rate;\n            *value += noise - (self.exploration_rate / 2.0);\n        }\n        Ok(new_cfg)\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// ---------- Factory Pattern: TunerFactory --------------------------------------------------------\n//\n\n/// Available tuning strategy identifiers.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Eq, PartialEq)]\npub enum TunerKind {\n    GridSearch,\n    Bayesian,\n}\n\n/// Factory responsible for creating boxed [`HyperparameterTuner`]s.\npub struct TunerFactory;\n\nimpl TunerFactory {\n    pub fn create(kind: TunerKind) -> Box<dyn HyperparameterTuner> {\n        match kind {\n            TunerKind::GridSearch => Box::new(GridSearchTuner::new(0.05)),\n            TunerKind::Bayesian => Box::new(BayesianTuner::new(0.10)),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// ---------- Observer Pattern: PerformanceMonitor -------------------------------------------------\n//\n\n/// Trait for observers interested in metric updates.\npub trait MetricsObserver: Send {\n    /// Called whenever new training metrics are recorded.\n    fn on_metrics_update(&mut self, metrics: &TrainingMetrics);\n}\n\n/// Concrete observer that monitors metrics & pushes [`RetrainEvent`]s.\npub struct PerformanceMonitor {\n    history: VecDeque<TrainingMetrics>,\n    window: usize,\n    threshold_pct: f32,\n    tx_retrain: Sender<RetrainEvent>,\n    model_id: String,\n}\n\nimpl PerformanceMonitor {\n    /// Create a new monitor.\n    ///\n    /// * `window` – number of recent metrics used to compute rolling mean.\n    /// * `threshold_pct` – acceptable degradation percentage vs. best.\n    pub fn new(\n        model_id: String,\n        window: usize,\n        threshold_pct: f32,\n        tx_retrain: Sender<RetrainEvent>,\n    ) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            history: VecDeque::with_capacity(window),\n            window,\n            threshold_pct,\n            tx_retrain,\n            model_id,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Computes moving average loss over the internal window.\n    fn avg_loss(&self) -> Option<f32> {\n        if self.history.is_empty() {\n            return None;\n        }\n        Some(\n            self.history\n                .iter()\n                .map(|m| m.loss)\n                .sum::<f32>()\n                / self.history.len() as f32,\n        )\n    }\n}\n\nimpl MetricsObserver for PerformanceMonitor {\n    fn on_metrics_update(&mut self, metrics: &TrainingMetrics) {\n        // Maintain bounded window.\n        if self.history.len() == self.window {\n            self.history.pop_front();\n        }\n        self.history.push_back(*metrics);\n\n        // Compute rolling avg and detect degradation.\n        if let Some(avg_loss) = self.avg_loss() {\n            let worst_allowed = avg_loss * (1.0 + self.threshold_pct);\n            if metrics.loss > worst_allowed {\n                // Push retrain event.\n                let evt = RetrainEvent {\n                    model_id: self.model_id.clone(),\n                    reason: format!(\n                        \"Loss degraded {:.2}% vs. rolling mean\",\n                        (metrics.loss / avg_loss - 1.0) * 100.0\n                    ),\n                };\n                // It's okay to ignore send errors — they indicate closed consumer.\n                let _ = self.tx_retrain.send(evt);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// ---------- Orchestration Boilerplate ------------------------------------------------------------\n//\n\n/// Coordinator that glues together tuner, observer, and async channels.\n///\n/// It represents a coarse-grained task that would likely run in a Tokio\n/// executor in the real orchestrator; here we model it with plain threads\n/// for minimal dependencies.\n///\n/// The loop:\n/// 1. Consumes new metrics from `rx_metrics`.\n/// 2. Feeds them to the tuner.\n/// 3. Broadcasts them to observers.\n/// 4. Emits new hyper-parameter configs via `tx_config`.\npub struct TuningCoordinator {\n    tuner: Mutex<Box<dyn HyperparameterTuner>>,\n    observers: Mutex<Vec<Box<dyn MetricsObserver>>>,\n    rx_metrics: Receiver<(HyperParameterConfig, TrainingMetrics)>,\n    tx_config: Sender<HyperParameterConfig>,\n}\n\nimpl TuningCoordinator {\n    #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]\n    pub fn new(\n        tuner: Box<dyn HyperparameterTuner>,\n        observers: Vec<Box<dyn MetricsObserver>>,\n        rx_metrics: Receiver<(HyperParameterConfig, TrainingMetrics)>,\n        tx_config: Sender<HyperParameterConfig>,\n    ) -> Arc<Self> {\n        Arc::new(Self {\n            tuner: Mutex::new(tuner),\n            observers: Mutex::new(observers),\n            rx_metrics,\n            tx_config,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Blocking loop; should be spawned on its own thread / executor.\n    pub fn start(self: Arc<Self>) {\n        std::thread::spawn(move || {\n            let ticker = tick(Duration::from_secs(1));\n\n            loop {\n                select! {\n                    recv(self.rx_metrics) -> msg => {\n                        match msg {\n                            Ok((cfg, metrics)) => {\n                                // 1) Produce new config via tuner.\n                                let mut tuner = self.tuner.lock().expect(\"poisoned lock\");\n                                match tuner.tune(&cfg, &metrics) {\n                                    Ok(new_cfg) => {\n                                        // Send back to upstream training service\n                                        if let Err(err) = self.tx_config.send(new_cfg) {\n                                            eprintln!(\"TuningCoordinator: failed to send new config: {err}\");\n                                        }\n                                    }\n                                    Err(e) => eprintln!(\"TuningCoordinator: tune() failed: {e:?}\"),\n                                }\n\n                                // 2) Notify observers.\n                                for obs in self.observers.lock().expect(\"poisoned lock\").iter_mut() {\n                                    obs.on_metrics_update(&metrics);\n                                }\n                            }\n                            Err(_) => {\n                                // Upstream closed. Shutdown gracefully.\n                                break;\n                            }\n                        }\n                    }\n                    recv(ticker) -> _ => {\n                        // Heart-beat for logging / metrics integration.\n                        // In prod we would emit metrics to Prometheus here.\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        });\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// ---------- Self-contained Integration Test -------------------------------------------------------\n//\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn end_to_end_flow() {\n        // a) Wire channels.\n        let (tx_metrics, rx_metrics) = bounded::<(HyperParameterConfig, TrainingMetrics)>(4);\n        let (tx_cfg_out, rx_cfg_out) = bounded::<HyperParameterConfig>(4);\n        let (tx_retrain, _rx_retrain) = bounded::<RetrainEvent>(4);\n\n        // b) Concrete strategy + observer.\n        let tuner = TunerFactory::create(TunerKind::GridSearch);\n        let monitor = PerformanceMonitor::new(\"model-xyz\".into(), 4, 0.05, tx_retrain);\n\n        // c) Coordinator.\n        let coord = TuningCoordinator::new(tuner, vec![Box::new(monitor)], rx_metrics, tx_cfg_out);\n        coord.start();\n\n        // d) Push dummy metrics.\n        let mut cfg = HyperParameterConfig::new();\n        cfg.insert(\"lr\", 0.01);\n        cfg.insert(\"momentum\", 0.9);\n\n        for i in 0..5 {\n            let mut metrics = TrainingMetrics {\n                accuracy: 0.9,\n                f1_score: 0.92,\n                loss: 0.1 + i as f32 * 0.02,\n            };\n            tx_metrics.send((cfg.clone(), metrics)).unwrap();\n\n            // Receive tuned config.\n            let new_cfg = rx_cfg_out.recv().unwrap();\n            assert!(new_cfg.get(\"lr\").is_some());\n        }\n    }\n}\n```","numLines":396,"startLine":1,"totalLines":396}}}

Using `serde_json::Value` keeps the design flexible without\n/// forcing consumers into yet another strongly typed DSL.\npub type HyperParams = HashMap<String, serde_json::Value>;\n\n/// Result experiment_id: uuid::Uuid,\n    pub params: HyperParams,\n    pub metrics: HashMap<String, f64>,\n    pub succeeded: bool,\n}\n\n/// Trait defining the contract for an adaptive hyper-parameter tuner.\n#[async_trait::async_trait]\npub trait HyperParamTuner: Send + Sync {\n    /// Returns a new candidate configuration for evaluation.\n    async fn suggest(&self) -> Result<HyperParams, TunerError>;\n\n    /// Updates the tuner’s internal state with the trial outcome so it can\n    /// refine subsequent suggestions.\n    async fn update(&self, result: TrialResult) -> Result<(), TunerError>;\n}\n\n/// An event emitted by the tuning engine.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub enum TunerEvent {\n    /// Emitted when a new suggestion is produced.\n    Suggestion {\n        experiment_id: uuid::Uuid,\n        params: HyperParams,\n    },\n    /// Emitted when a trial result has been ingested.\n    TrialCompleted(TrialResult),\n}\n\nimpl Display for TunerEvent {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        match self {\n            TunerEvent::Suggestion {\n                experiment_id,\n                params,\n            } => write!(\n                f,\n                \"New suggestion {experiment_id}: {} params\",\n                params.len()\n            ),\n            TunerEvent::TrialCompleted(result) => write!(\n                f,\n                \"Trial {} completed — success: {}\",\n                result.experiment_id, result.succeeded\n            ),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Observer trait enabling plug-ins to watch the tuner in real-time.\n///\n/// Example implementations:\n/// * `DashboardSink`  – pushes events to a web socket.\n/// * `AlertingSink`   – triggers alerts on regression.\n/// * `RegistrySink`   – logs completed trials to the Model Registry.\n#[async_trait::async_trait]\npub trait TunerObserver: Send + Sync {\n    async fn on_event(&self, event: TunerEvent);\n}\n\n/// Concrete bus implementation used for decoupled fan-out.\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct EventBus {\n    sender: broadcast::Sender<TunerEvent>,\n}\n\nimpl EventBus {\n    pub fn new(capacity: usize) -> Self {\n        let (sender, _) = broadcast::channel(capacity);\n        Self { sender }\n    }\n\n    pub fn subscribe(&self) -> broadcast::Receiver<TunerEvent> {\n        self.sender.subscribe()\n    }\n\n    pub fn publish(&self, event: TunerEvent) -> Result<(), TunerError> {\n        self.sender\n            .send(event)\n            .map(|_| ())\n            .map_err(|e| TunerError::PublishError(e.into()))\n    }\n}\n\n/// Simple Bayesian-like tuner with random exploration.\n//\n/// NOTE: Production systems would integrate libraries like GPyTorch or\n/// optuna-rs.  This stub is *probabilistic* for illustration while remaining\n/// self-contained.\npub struct BayesianTuner {\n    state: RwLock<TunerState>,\n    event_bus: EventBus,\n}\n\n/// Internal mutable state protected by an `RwLock`.\nstruct TunerState {\n    trials: Vec<TrialResult>,\n}\n\nimpl BayesianTuner {\n    pub fn new(event_bus: EventBus) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            state: RwLock::new(TunerState { trials: vec![] }),\n            event_bus,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Register a new observer that receives all subsequent events.\n    pub fn register_observer<O>(&self, observer: Arc<O>)\n    where\n        O: TunerObserver + 'static,\n    {\n        let mut rx = self.event_bus.subscribe();\n        tokio::spawn(async move {\n            while let Ok(event) = rx.recv().await {\n                observer.on_event(event).await;\n            }\n        });\n    }\n\n    /// Internal helper to emit and swallow any publication error.\n    async fn emit(&self, event: TunerEvent) {\n        if let Err(e) = self.event_bus.publish(event.clone()) {\n            tracing::error!(\"Failed to publish event {event:?}: {e}\");\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait::async_trait]\nimpl HyperParamTuner for BayesianTuner {\n    async fn suggest(&self) -> Result<HyperParams, TunerError> {\n        // Very naïve: choose random learning rate and depth.\n        let mut rng = rand::thread_rng();\n        let lr: f64 = rng.gen_range(1e-5..1e-1);\n        let depth: u64 = rng.gen_range(18..152); // ResNet-like depths.\n\n        let mut params = HyperParams::new();\n        params.insert(\"optimizer.lr\".into(), serde_json::json!(lr));\n        params.insert(\"model.depth\".into(), serde_json::json!(depth));\n\n        let experiment_id = uuid::Uuid::new_v4();\n        self.emit(TunerEvent::Suggestion {\n            experiment_id,\n            params: params.clone(),\n        })\n        .await;\n        Ok(params)\n    }\n\n    async fn update(&self, result: TrialResult) -> Result<(), TunerError> {\n        {\n            let mut state = self.state.write().await;\n            state.trials.push(result.clone());\n        }\n        self.emit(TunerEvent::TrialCompleted(result)).await;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// Rich domain errors that bubble up to callers.\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum TunerError {\n    #[error(\"failed to fetch suggestion: {0}\")]\n    SuggestionError(String),\n\n    #[error(\"failed to ingest trial result: {0}\")]\n    UpdateError(String),\n\n    #[error(\"event bus publish error: {0}\")]\n    PublishError(#[source] anyhow::Error),\n}\n\n/// Convenience alias for a shared tuner handle.\npub type SharedTuner = Arc<dyn HyperParamTuner>;\n\n/// Example observer that logs every event via `tracing`.\n///\n/// In production, this could push to a Prometheus endpoint or persistent\n/// storage.\npub struct LoggingObserver;\n\n#[async_trait::async_trait]\nimpl TunerObserver for LoggingObserver {\n    async fn on_event(&self, event: TunerEvent) {\n        match &event {\n            TunerEvent::Suggestion { .. } => tracing::info!(\"{event}\"),\n            TunerEvent::TrialCompleted(result) => {\n                tracing::info!(\"{event}; primary metric: {:?}\", result.metrics)\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Integration Helper\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Create a fully wired tuner with a default logging observer.\n///\n/// This helper can be invoked by orchestration code to keep the call-sites\n/// lean.\npub fn provision_default_tuner() -> SharedTuner {\n    let bus = EventBus::new(128);\n    let tuner = Arc::new(BayesianTuner::new(bus.clone())) as SharedTuner;\n\n    // Attach default observer.\n    let observer = Arc::new(LoggingObserver);\n    // Downcast to concrete type to call extension method.\n    if let Some(concrete) = Arc::clone(&tuner)\n        .downcast_arc::<BayesianTuner>()\n        .ok()\n    {\n        concrete.register_observer(observer);\n    }\n\n    tuner\n}\n\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Unit Tests\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use std::time::Duration;\n\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn smoke_test_tuner() {\n        tracing_subscriber::fmt::init();\n        let tuner = provision_default_tuner();\n\n        // Generate suggestion and feed a dummy result.\n        let params = tuner.suggest().await.unwrap();\n        assert!(params.contains_key(\"optimizer.lr\"));\n\n        let trial = TrialResult {\n            experiment_id: uuid::Uuid::new_v4(),\n            params,\n            metrics: [(\"accuracy\".into(), 0.75)].into(),\n            succeeded: true,\n        };\n        tuner.update(trial).await.unwrap();\n\n        // Wait briefly to allow observer to log.\n        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(50)).await;\n    }\n}\n```","numLines":291,"startLine":1,"totalLines":291}}}

That responsibility\n    45→/// lies with the `Model Ops` layer.  The tuner is also side-effect free––\n    46→/// apart from internal bookkeeping.\n    47→pub trait HyperparameterTuner: Send + Sync {\n    48→    /// Suggest the next configuration to evaluate.\n    49→    fn suggest(&mut self) -> Option<HyperParams>;\n    50→\n    51→    /// Report the evaluation metric (e.g. accuracy, loss) for a previously\n    52→    /// suggested configuration.  Implementations may choose to ignore the\n    53→    /// score when the configuration is unknown.\n    54→    fn report_result(&mut self, params: &HyperParams, metric: f64);\n    55→\n    56→    /// Human-readable name; useful for telemetry & debugging.\n    57→    fn name(&self) -> &'static str;\n    58→}\n    59→\n    60→/// A simple grid-search tuner that exhaustively evaluates the Cartesian\n    61→/// product of discrete candidate values.\n    62→///\n    63→/// Useful for deterministic sweeps when the search space is small.\n    64→pub struct GridSearchTuner {\n    65→    grid: Vec<HyperParams>,\n    66→    cursor: usize,\n    67→}\n    68→\n    69→impl GridSearchTuner {\n    70→    pub fn new(candidates: HashMap<String, Vec<f64>>) -> Self {\n    71→        // Compute            &candidates.into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>(),\n    77→            0,\n    78→        );\n    79→        info!(\"GridSearch initialized with {} configurations\", grid.len());\n    80→        Self { grid, cursor: 0 }\n    81→    }\n    82→\n    83→    fn cartesian_product(\n    84→        acc: &mut Vec<HyperParams>,\n    85→        current: &mut HyperParams,\n    86→        items: &[(String, Vec<f64>)],\n    87→        idx: usize,\n    88→    ) {\n    89→        if idx == items.len() {\n    90→            acc.push(current.clone());\n    91→            return;\n    92→        }\n    93→\n    94→        let (ref key, ref values) = items[idx];\n    95→        for v in values {\n    96→            current.insert(key.clone(), *v);\n    97→            Self::cartesian_product(acc, current, items, idx + 1);\n    98→        }\n    99→        current.remove(key);\n   100→    }\n   101→}\n   102→\n   103→impl HyperparameterTuner for GridSearchTuner {\n   104→    fn suggest(&mut self) -> Option<HyperParams> {\n   105→        if self.cursor >= self.grid.len() {\n   106→            None\n   107→        } else {\n   108→            let params = self.grid[self.cursor].clone();\n   109→            self.cursor += 1;\n   110→            Some(params)\n   111→        }\n   112→    }\n   113→\n   114→    fn report_result(&mut self, _params: 120→    }\n   121→}\n   122→\n   123→/// Random search tuner that samples uniformly from user-supplied ranges.\n   124→/// Each range is inclusive.\n   125→///\n   126→/// NOTE: For continuous       \"RandomSearchTuner\"\n   171→    }\n   172→}\n   173→\n   174→/// Factory pattern: build tuners dynamically based on a string identifier.\n   175→/// This allows runtime configuration via CLI, The tuner implementation itself can be an\n   193→/// observer in order to implement adaptive search algorithms such as Bayesian\n   194→/// optimization, however, we keep them decoupled so that   },\n   207→}\n   208→\n   209→/// Observer trait.\n   210→pub trait TrainingObserver: Send + Sync {\n   211→    fn on_event(&self, event: &TrainingEvent);\n   212→}\n   213→\n   214→/// Default candidates.insert(\"learning_rate\".into(), vec![0.01, 0.001]);\n   357→    candidates.insert(\"dropout\".into(), vec![0.1, 0.2, 0.3]);\n   358→\n   359→    let tuner: Arc<Mutex<Box<dyn That responsibility\n/// lies with the `Model Ops` layer.  The tuner is also side-effect free––\n/// apart from internal bookkeeping.\npub trait HyperparameterTuner: Send + Sync {\n    /// Suggest the next configuration to evaluate.\n    fn suggest(&mut self) -> Option<HyperParams>;\n\n    /// Report the evaluation metric (e.g. accuracy, loss) for a previously\n    /// suggested configuration.  Implementations may choose to ignore the\n    /// score when the configuration is unknown.\n    fn report_result(&mut self, params: &HyperParams, metric: f64);\n\n    /// Human-readable name; useful for telemetry & debugging.\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str;\n}\n\n/// A simple grid-search tuner that exhaustively evaluates the Cartesian\n/// product of discrete candidate values.\n///\n/// Useful for deterministic sweeps when the search space is small.\npub struct GridSearchTuner {\n    grid: Vec<HyperParams>,\n    cursor: usize,\n}\n\nimpl GridSearchTuner {\n    pub fn new(candidates: HashMap<String, Vec<f64>>) -> Self {\n        // Compute Cartesian product\n        let mut grid: Vec<HyperParams> = vec![];\n        Self::cartesian_product(\n            &mut grid,\n            &mut HyperParams::new(),\n            &candidates.into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>(),\n            0,\n        );\n        info!(\"GridSearch initialized with {} configurations\", grid.len());\n        Self { grid, cursor: 0 }\n    }\n\n    fn cartesian_product(\n        acc: &mut Vec<HyperParams>,\n        current: &mut HyperParams,\n        items: &[(String, Vec<f64>)],\n        idx: usize,\n    ) {\n        if idx == items.len() {\n            acc.push(current.clone());\n            return;\n        }\n\n        let (ref key, ref values) = items[idx];\n        for v in values {\n            current.insert(key.clone(), *v);\n            Self::cartesian_product(acc, current, items, idx + 1);\n        }\n        current.remove(key);\n    }\n}\n\nimpl HyperparameterTuner for GridSearchTuner {\n    fn suggest(&mut self) -> Option<HyperParams> {\n        if self.cursor >= self.grid.len() {\n            None\n        } else {\n            let params = self.grid[self.cursor].clone();\n            self.cursor += 1;\n            Some(params)\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn report_result(&mut self, _params: &HyperParams, _metric: f64) {\n        // grid search is stateless; nothing to do.\n    }\n\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"GridSearchTuner\"\n    }\n}\n\n/// Random search tuner that samples uniformly from user-supplied ranges.\n/// Each range is inclusive.\n///\n/// NOTE: For continuous variables, the range is treated as continuous;\n/// for discrete variables, the caller can pass equal min==max to achieve\n/// deterministic behavior.\npub struct RandomSearchTuner {\n    ranges: HashMap<String, (f64, f64)>,\n    max_trials: usize,\n    trials: usize,\n    history: Vec<(HyperParams, f64)>,\n}\n\nimpl RandomSearchTuner {\n    pub fn new(ranges: HashMap<String, (f64, f64)>, max_trials: usize) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            ranges,\n            max_trials,\n            trials: 0,\n            history: Vec::new(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn random_sample(&self) -> HyperParams {\n        let mut rng = thread_rng();\n        self.ranges\n            .iter()\n            .map(|(k, (min, max))| (k.clone(), rng.gen_range(*min..=*max)))\n            .collect()\n    }\n}\n\nimpl HyperparameterTuner for RandomSearchTuner {\n    fn suggest(&mut self) -> Option<HyperParams> {\n        if self.trials >= self.max_trials {\n            None\n        } else {\n            self.trials += 1;\n            Some(self.random_sample())\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn report_result(&mut self, params: &HyperParams, metric: f64) {\n        self.history.push((params.clone(), metric));\n    }\n\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"RandomSearchTuner\"\n    }\n}\n\n/// Factory pattern: build tuners dynamically based on a string identifier.\n/// This allows runtime configuration via CLI, YAML, or remote control plane.\npub enum TunerFactory;\n\nimpl TunerFactory {\n    pub fn make_grid_search(candidates: HashMap<String, Vec<f64>>) -> Box<dyn HyperparameterTuner> {\n        Box::new(GridSearchTuner::new(candidates))\n    }\n\n    pub fn make_random_search(\n        ranges: HashMap<String, (f64, f64)>,\n        max_trials: usize,\n    ) -> Box<dyn HyperparameterTuner> {\n        Box::new(RandomSearchTuner::new(ranges, max_trials))\n    }\n}\n\n/// Observer pattern – The model-training engine emits `TrainingEvent`s that\n/// observers can subscribe to.  The tuner implementation itself can be an\n/// observer in order to implement adaptive search algorithms such as Bayesian\n/// optimization, however, we keep them decoupled so that multiple observers\n/// (e.g. loggers, alerting) can co-exist.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub enum TrainingEvent {\n    EpochStart { epoch: u32 },\n    EpochEnd {\n        epoch: u32,\n        metrics: HashMap<String, f64>,\n    },\n    TrainingFinish {\n        best_metric: f64,\n        best_params: HyperParams,\n    },\n}\n\n/// Observer trait.\npub trait TrainingObserver: Send + Sync {\n    fn on_event(&self, event: &TrainingEvent);\n}\n\n/// Default implementation that feeds back the final metric to the tuner.\npub struct TunerObserver {\n    tuner: Arc<Mutex<Box<dyn HyperparameterTuner>>>,\n}\n\nimpl TunerObserver {\n    pub fn new(tuner: Arc<Mutex<Box<dyn HyperparameterTuner>>>) -> Self {\n        Self { tuner }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl TrainingObserver for TunerObserver {\n    fn on_event(&self, event: &TrainingEvent) {\n        if let TrainingEvent::TrainingFinish {\n            best_metric,\n            best_params,\n        } = event\n        {\n            if let Ok(mut tuner) = self.tuner.lock() {\n                tuner.report_result(best_params, *best_metric);\n            } else {\n                error!(\"Unable to lock tuner mutex in observer\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Lightweight experiment tracking backed by SQLite.\n/// Production deployments can replace this with a more\n/// sophisticated store (Postgres, Deserialize)]\npub struct ExperimentRecord {\n    pub id: i64,\n    pub timestamp: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub tuner_name: String,\n    pub params: HyperParams,\n    pub metric: f64,\n}\n\nimpl ExperimentTracker {\n    /// Create a new tracker with the given file path.\n    pub fn new<P: AsRef<Path>>(db_path: P) -> Result<Self> {\n        let conn = Connection::open(&db_path)\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"opening experiments db {:?}\", db_path.as_ref()))?;\n\n        let tracker = Self {\n            db_path: db_path.as_ref().to_path_buf(),\n            conn,\n        };\n\n hyperparams\")?;\n\n        self.conn.execute(\n            \"\n            INSERT INTO experiments (timestamp, tuner_name, params, metric)\n            VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3, ?4);\n            \",\n            params![\n                timestamp.to_rfc3339(),\n                tuner_name,\n                params_blob,\n                metric\n            ],\n        )?;\n\n        Ok(self.conn.last_insert_rowid())\n    }\n\n    /// Retrieve all experiments for auditing or analytics.\n    pub fn all_records(&self) -> Result<Vec<ExperimentRecord>> {\n        let mut stmt = self.conn.prepare(\n            \"\n            SELECT id, timestamp, tuner_name, params, metric\n            FROM experiments\n            ORDER BY id ASC;\n            \",\n        )?;\n\n        let iter = stmt.query_map([], |row| {\n            let params_blob: Vec<u8> = row.get(3)?;\n            let params: HyperParams = serde_json::from_slice(&params_blob)\n                .map_err(|e| rusqlite::Error::FromSqlConversionFailure(3, rusqlite::types::Type::Blob, Box::new(e)))?;\n\n            Ok(ExperimentRecord {\n                id: row.get(0)?,\n                timestamp: DateTime::parse_from_rfc3339(&row.get::<_, String>(1)?)\n                    .map(|dt| dt.with_timezone(&Utc))\n                    .map_err(|e| rusqlite::Error::FromSqlConversionFailure(1, rusqlite::types::Type::Text, Box::new(e)))?,\n                tuner_name: row.get(2)?,\n                params,\n                metric: row.get(4)?,\n            })\n        })?;\n\n        let mut out = Vec::new();\n        for rec in iter {\n            out.push(rec?);\n        }\n        Ok(out)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Example of orchestrating an end-to-end hyper-parameter tuning session.\n/// The function is *sync* for brevity but can be made async using\n/// `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` or similar.\n///\n/// NOTE: This is *not* executed automatically; it serves as a reference\n/// implementation for higher-level orchestration code.\npub fn run_hpo_session() -> Result<()> {\n    // ------------------------------------------\n    // Create the tuner (here: grid search)\n    // ------------------------------------------\n    let mut candidates: HashMap<String, Vec<f64>> = HashMap::new();\n    candidates.insert(\"learning_rate\".into(), vec![0.01, 0.001]);\n    candidates.insert(\"dropout\".into(), vec![0.1, 0.2, 0.3]);\n\n    let tuner: Arc<Mutex<Box<dyn HyperparameterTuner>>> =\n        Arc::new(Mutex::new(TunerFactory::make_grid_search(candidates)));\n\n    // ------------------------------------------\n    // Initialize experiment tracker\n    // ------------------------------------------\n    let tracker = info!(\"Hyper-parameter search complete.\");\n            break;\n        };\n\n        // ------------------------------------------------------------------\n        // >>>>  In the real system, spawn_dispatch_loop(\n        monitor: Arc<ModelMonitor>,\n        mut rx: UnboundedReceiver<ModelEvent>,\n        _buffer: usize,\n    ) {\n        task::spawn(async move {\n            while let Some(event) = rx.recv().await {\n                monitor.notify(event);\n            }\n        });\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Subject for ModelMonitor {\n    fn register_observer(&mut self, obs: Arc<dyn obs_id < guard.len() {\n            guard.remove(obs_id);\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn notify(&self, event: ModelEvent) {\n        let guard = self\n            .observers\n            .read()\n            .expect(\"ModelMonitor observer lock poisoned\");\n        for obs in guard.iter() {\n            // Fire & forget; each observer must handle its own failure modes\n            let evt =            .create(true)\n            .append(true)\n            .open(&self.log_path)?\n            .write_all(format!(\"{experiment_id},{json}\\n\").as_bytes())?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// HyperparamTuningStrategy>,\n    pub tracker: Arc<ExperimentTracker>,\n    pub dataset_path: PathBuf,\n}\n\nimpl Observer for RetrainTrigger {\n    fn on_event(&self, event: &ModelEvent) -> Result<(), This module lives inside the `Feature Ops / Model Ops` layers\n     6→//! and is responsible for proposing hyper-parameter candidates, capturing their\n     7→//! evaluation metrics, and broadcasting events that can be consumed by other\n     8→//! system components (e.g. the automated-retraining daemon).\n     9→//!\n    10→//! Architectural patterns used\n    11→//! ---------------------------\n    12→//! • Strategy Pattern – concrete `Tuner` implementations (Grid, Random, …) share\n    13→//!   a common `Tuner` trait.\n    14→//! • Factory Pattern  – `TunerFactory` produces a concrete strategy at runtime\n    15→//!   from high-level configuration.\n    16→//! • Observer Pattern – `EventBus` enables decoupled metric listeners.\n    17→//!\n    18→//! External crates (add to Cargo.toml)\n    19→//! -----------------------------------\n    20→//! rand        = \"0.8\"\n    21→//! serde       = { version = \"1\", features = [\"derive\"] }\n    22→//! thiserror   = \"1\"\n    23→//! chrono      = { version = \"0.4\", features = [\"serde\"] }\n    24→//! log         = \"0.4\"\n    25→//! parking_lot = \"0.12\"\n    26→//! -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    27→//! NOTE:  This file purposefully avoids referencing any “deep-learny” code; the\n    28→//!        idea is that a caller feeds candidates to a model-training routine\n    29→//!        and then invokes `update_result` with an obtained metric.\n    30→//! Each parameter maps to a set of allowed\n    85→/// values. Returns `false` if wrapped-around\n    /// and the entire grid was already enumerated.\n    fn increment_cursor(&mut self) -> bool {\n        for (dim_idx, (_, values)) in self.space.iter().enumerate().rev() {\n            let limit = values.len();\n            self.cursor[dim_idx] += 1;\n            if self.cursor[dim_idx] < limit {\n                return true; //
